## Ground-based Midcourse Defense

Boeing is the system developer and prime contractor for the Ground-based Midcourse Defense (GMD) system, the U.S.'s only defense against long-range ballistic missiles. GMD consists of networked space-, sea- and land-based sensors; command and control facilities; and ground-based interceptors (GBI) connected through an extensive space and terrestrial communications network. The system has achieved 12 successful intercepts in 19 tests. Boeing continues to lead GMD development, integration, testing, operations and sustainment activities. In 2018, Boeing was awarded a $6.5 billion agreement to extend the GMD development and sustainment contract through December 2023. In 2020, the Boeing-led team delivered 13 silos and silo interface vaults to Fort Greely, Alaska, nearly eight months ahead of the delivery schedule. In 2021, the Boeing-led team, alongside the U.S. Missile Defense Agency, demonstrated an advanced missile defense capability through a successful flight test. The test included launching a GBI into space with a three-stage booster in twostage mode, allowing the GBI to release the kill vehicle earlier in flight.

Boeing is redefining the boundaries of military rotorcraft. Driven by proven platforms and the latest technology, Boeing supports the vital missions of defense forces around the world. Boeing military rotorcraft include the medium- to heavy-lift cargo helicopter H-47 Chinook; the multimission attack helicopter AH-64 Apache; the light attack and reconnaissance AH-6 Little Bird; the V-22 Osprey, a multirole combat tiltrotor aircraft combining the vertical performance of a helicopter with the speed and range of a fixed-wing aircraft; and, most recently, the multimission utility helicopter MH-139A Grey Wolf. Trusted and in demand by the U.S. Army, Navy, Air Force and Marines and by allied defense forces in more than 20 countries, Boeing rotorcraft products ensure that troops are prepared for the toughest missions-today and tomorrow.

In 2022, Boeing achieved two key international vertical lift down-select awards. In June, Germany selected the Chinook (60 aircraft) as its future heavy-lift aircraft,

Ocean on Dec. 11. Work is well underway at NASA's Michoud Assembly Facility (MAF) in Louisiana on the core stage for Artemis II, which is slated to be the first crewed mission. Core Stage 2 is expected to be delivered to KSC in 2023. Core stage structures for the Artemis III and IV missions, which aim to land the first woman and person of color on the lunar surface, are currently in production. In addition, Boeing is building the new, more powerful Exploration Upper Stage to power astronauts and cargo to the moon and beyond in a single launch, replacing the Boeing- and United Alliance-built Interim Cryogenic Propulsion Stage used for the first evolution of the rocket.

## Minuteman III Intercontinental Ballistic Missile

Since 1958, Boeing has designed, developed, delivered and sustained three generations of Minuteman intercontinental ballistic missiles (ICBM) for the U.S. Air Force as a foundational partner in our nation's mission of strategic deterrence. In 2017, the Air Force awarded Boeing more than $330 million for sustainment and modernization efforts across the nuclear triad. In 2021, the Air Force awarded Boeing $1.6 billion to continue guidance and navigation repair efforts for the Minuteman ICBM through 2039. Today, hundreds of Boeing employees across the country continue to maintain the readiness of the Minuteman ICBM-keeping it safe, secure and effective around the clock. Boeing looks forward to continuing our partnership with the Air Force-keeping the Minuteman ICBM mission-ready today while delivering innovative solutions to support strategic deterrence tomorrow.

## Commercial Satellites and Services

Boeing Commercial Satellites designs and sells communications satellites and payloads for commercial telecommunications, broadband, scientific and environmental applications. Boeing-built spacecraft relay voice and video communications, streaming video content and direct-to-home entertainment.

Forging the path for connectivity, Boeing's development of O3b mPOWER, an 11-satellite constellation for satellite operator SES, will provide high-performance data communications to users around the world. With more than 5,000 steerable beams that can reallocate bandwidth on demand, the O3b mPOWER satellite is truly the first of its kind. Boeing delivered the first two of these satellites during the fourth quarter of 2022. Boeing also delivered two C-band satellites to SES in 2022, marking the first satellite deliveries for Boeing since 2019.

For ViaSat, Boeing is building three 702 satellite platforms that will be integrated with ViaSat-3 payloads, slated to deliver industryleading boosts to residential services as well as improved video streaming capability for commercial and business jet aircraft, and high-value government transport. Boeing works closely with subsidiary Millennium Space Systems on small satellite constellation offerings. Spectrolab's solar cells power some of the most iconic space missions and satellites, including the O3b mPOWER constellation, the International Space Station's new roll-out arrays, and a number of cubesats deployed during the Artemis I mission.

## Government Satellite Systems

Boeing Government Satellite Systems delivers satellite and ground solutions that support government customers' national security operations, including intelligence, surveillance, reconnaissance and communications.

Moving at mission-relevant speed to deliver resilient systems to the escalating threat environment, Boeing leverages technological innovation from commercial and government space programs developing best-in-class satellite systems across all orbits and mission sets. With capabilities ranging from payload technology development to designing turnkey end-to-end systems, our products encompass narrowband ultra-highfrequency solutions that enable worldwide communication in challenging environments and protected wideband systems that enable high-capacity broadband connectivity for the U.S. government and its allies.

Boeing made marked progress on a number of Tactical Military Satellite Communication (MILSATCOM) programs in 2022, including launching production for the 11th Wideband Global SATCOM (WGS) satellite, which will join the constellation that underpins the DOD and allied connectivity. WGS-11+, currently expected to deliver in 2024, brings advanced capability and resilience to the warfighter. On rapid prototyping programs for the U.S. Space Force that are designed to deliver critical capability in shorter time frames, Boeing continued to demonstrate and iterate on both space-based and groundbased anti-jam technology, the Protected Tactical SATCOM Prototype (PTS-P) and Protected Tactical Enterprise Service (PTES), respectively. These programs are critical to ensuring warfighter connectivity in contested environments.

A leader in advanced space experimentation, Boeing is the prime contractor for the X-37B Orbital Test Vehicle-the most advanced uncrewed reentry spacecraft used by the U.S. Air Force to perform risk-reduction and experimentation for reusable space vehicle technologies. The spaceplane successfully completed its sixth mission in 2022, returning to Earth after spending more than 900 days on orbit, shattering its previous endurance record.

## Boeing Defense, Space &amp; Security

continued

Boeing provides a high-tech portfolio of precision-guided weapons, cruise missile systems, laser weapon systems and integrated air and missile defense solutions to the DOD and more than 40 international partners, with significant investments to increase capacity and advance capability, including Joint Direct Attack Munition, Small Diameter Bomb, Harpoon, Patriot Advanced Capability-3 (PAC-3) Missile seeker and Standoff Land Attack Missile Expanded Response (SLAM ER).

In 2021, Boeing completed construction of a new 35,000-square-foot manufacturing facility to support increased production for cruise missile systems programs. It also received multiple contracts to continue to meet warfighter needs in 2022, including a High Altitude Anti-Submarine Warfare Weapon Capability (HAAWC) full-rate production contract to help meet the U.S. Navy's operational need to conduct antisubmarine warfare from higher altitudes and longer distances. In addition, Boeing delivered its 4,500th PAC-3 seeker in 2022 and is nearing completion of an additional 9,000-square-foot facilities expansion to support growing air and missile defense needs.

| ## Digital Aviation Solutions  Digital Aviation Solutions combines data analysis and engineering know-how to deliver savings and efficiencies for customers, operators, technicians and maintainers. Boeing analytics experts and engineers help customers solve problems by transforming troves of aircraft and system data into actionable insights to enable higher reliability, readiness and performance while reducing costs. Specifically for our government  customers, these services are delivered through our Next Generation Product Support portfolio.  Across the commercial, defense, business aviation and general aviation markets, Boeing's extensive collection of digital products and services collect, analyze and leverage insights to improve operational safety, reliability and efficiency; reduce fuel consumption and emissions; maximize quality in maintenance work; and lower operating costs regardless of platform manufacturer.  ## Parts &amp; Distribution Services  Parts &amp; Distribution Services operates one of the industry's most comprehensive aftermarket parts and services networks. Whether customers need high-value parts, tailored solutions or consumables and expendables, Global Services ensures they have access to the world's most robust supply chain and the flexibility, scale and purchasing power to operate efficiently. Using supply chain expertise and advanced analytics capabilities, Boeing supports all aspects of fleet management, including airplane-on-ground support, component repair, used serviceable material offerings and engine- and component-exchange  programs that enable reliable and sustainable operations. Our original equipment manufacturer knowledge and distribution network delivers when it matters most for customers who expect stability, reliability and flexibility for their Boeing and non-Boeing platforms. Whether for in-production or out-of-production aircraft, Global Services understands material and logistics support, including spare parts, retrofit kits, distribution and life-cycle management, as well as performance-based logistics agreements.  ## Training Solutions  Training Solutions is a leader in aviation learning and simulator services, developing and delivering training programs, technology, simulation and analytics tools-and the software, technical services and data solutions, and training center support to go with it-to further enhance aviation safety as well as meet the career-long training  needs of pilots, maintainers and cabin crew in commercial, defense and early career aviation learning. Enabled by Boeing's global infrastructure, data-driven approach and research facilities, Training Solutions provides superior customer support and has evolved its world-class offerings into comprehensive solutions that further enhance aviation safety.  ## Engineering, Modifications &amp; Maintenance  Boeing Global Services commercial and defense customers around the world rely on our Engineering, Modifications &amp; Maintenance technical fleet solutions to optimize readiness and reliability. Unmatched industry innovation, agile execution and a global presence allow Boeing to convert, sustain and upgrade any type of aircraft flown today. ## Boeing Capital Corporation

Gregory L. Hyslop, Chief Engineer; Executive Vice President, Boeing Engineering, Test &amp; Technology, Seattle, Washington, USA

Boeing sees electric vertical takeoff and landing (eVTOL) aircraft as critical to enabling the future growth of the aerospace industry. This includes maturing important technologies, such as electric propulsion, autonomy and other capabilities that will be pioneered with eVTOL vehicles. At Boeing, we have our joint venture Wisk, which is working to become the first certified, self-flying eVTOL vehicle to market. In October 2022, Wisk revealed its 6th Generation aircraft, the world's first all-electric, autonomous, fourseat eVTOL air taxi designed for passenger transport. This vehicle will make it possible for passengers to skip the traffic and get to their destination faster. Vehicles such as this will enable new business opportunities centered around urban air mobility;

additionally, we are working with our airline customers to understand how such vehicles might integrate into their fleets, both for short-distance operations and to compliment more traditional operations with a finaldestination add-on service.

Wisk is an advanced air mobility company dedicated to delivering safe, everyday flight for everyone. Headquartered in the San Francisco Bay Area with locations around the world, Wisk is an independent company backed by The Boeing Company and Kitty Hawk Corporation. With over 12 years of experience and more than 1,600 flight tests, Wisk is shaping the future of daily commutes and urban travel, safely and sustainably.

Timothy R. Myers, President, Boeing Capital Corporation, Renton, Washington, USA

Boeing Capital Corporation, a wholly owned subsidiary of The Boeing Company, is a global provider of innovative financial solutions. Drawing on its comprehensive expertise, Boeing Capital Corporation ensures that a range of efficient financial options is available for customers of Boeing products and services.

For more than five decades, Boeing Capital Corporation has been instrumental in the development of the global aircraft financing infrastructure, and it paved the way for the emergence of modern operating lessors and banks. Today, with its unrivaled understanding of the commercial aviation industry and strong relationships with the financial, airline and lessor communities, Boeing Capital Corporation continues to develop and promote innovative financing structures as well as spearhead industry standards. As of Dec. 31, 2022, Boeing Capital Corporation managed a portfolio of approximately $1.5 billion.

Shareholders are encouraged to enroll in the electronic delivery program to receive all shareholder communications, including proxy voting materials, electronically instead of by mail. Registered shareholders can enroll at www.computershare.com/investor.

Registered shareholders with duplicate accounts may contact Computershare for instructions regarding the consolidation of those accounts. We recommend that registered shareholders always use the same form of their names in all stock transactions to be handled in the same account. Registered shareholders may also ask Computershare to eliminate excess mailings of annual reports going to shareholders in the same household.

## Connecting in Times of Need

Boeing employees in Poland helped migrating Ukrainian teammates earlier this year as they fled their native land. They donated and delivered food and medical supplies, set up food trucks and shelters and even opened their homes to their colleagues and strangers crossing over to rebuild new lives. Boeing committed 2 million euros to support humanitarian response efforts in Ukraine and doubled every dollar donated by employees-adding up to more than 1 million euros in charitable contributions.

'My mother and I took a few valuables and my cat and ended up in Poland after a 34-hour train ride. Our Polish colleagues really helped us. They opened their hearts to us, and we really appreciated it! During the first days of war, I must have received a thousand messages from people, friends and colleagues who I maybe met just one time. They were asking what they could do for us. It was really amazing. We feel this support even now.'

- -Olha Avdieienko (pictured left), originally from Kyiv, Ukraine, now located in Gdan ´sk, Poland

'Everybody in Poland did all they could to help. My boyfriend owns a food truck, so we decided to drive to the border and prepare food for people crossing the border as well as Boeing Ukraine employees, volunteers, soldiers, policemen and firemen. I ended up helping three families who stayed at my house in Warsaw. They cleaned up, ate dinner with us and slept safely before I helped them get to their new apartment in Gda´ nsk. I am so glad I had a chance to do that.'

- -Klaudia Bartkowiak (pictured far left), Boeing Poland, Warsaw
